# Privacy Engine I164 Accepted Mapping Evidence-Path Reclassification Determination V1

## 1. Status and Scope

This record is the immutable Engineering[171] successor evidence-path determination for the fixed I164 baseline.

It determines only the evidence-path consequence of the accepted Engineering[169] implementation-owner mapping declaration and the Engineering[170] acceptance occurrence.

STATE.md remains the single canonical development authority.

Protocol authority always remains with the published Privacy Engine protocol generation.

The preserved hierarchy is:

> Privacy Engine protocol authority  
> `!=` I164 implementation governance  
> `!=` Speak Up application meaning

The Privacy Engine remains the primary implementation-independent privacy participation protocol. I164 remains one bounded implementation baseline. Speak Up remains one application adapter and use case.

This record does not modify protocol semantics, Profile V2, I164, Engineering[166]–[170], any implementation material, or any requirement result.

## 2. Architectural Question and Rule

The bounded question is:

> Which exact A04–A08 rows now possess a complete evidence path because Engineering[170] accepted the Engineering[169] implementation-owner declaration, and which retain an unresolved complete formal relationship?

The applied rule is:

> exact Profile V2 row  
> `+` exact Engineering[167] ambiguity  
> `+` accepted Engineering[169] relationship or non-mapping  
> `+` exact I164 material  
> `+` limitations and dependencies  
> `+` Engineering[170] acceptance authority  
> `->` bounded evidence path only when the complete recorded ambiguity is resolved

Forbidden paths remain:

> partial component `->` complete relationship  
> accepted declaration `->` behaviour proof  
> bounded evidence path `->` evidence sufficiency  
> mapping evidence `->` requirement result

## 5. Acceptance Integrity and Boundary

Engineering[170] retains:

- acceptance status: accepted;
- accepting authority: implementation-governance boundary governing I164;
- acceptance-occurrence integrity identifier: `3c2a25b20c119d399cf3e954c26fd465834c82b6835b430f5375552fb1a1be06`;
- Private Actor identity encoded: no;
- requirement assessment performed: no;
- requirement result produced: no;
- Reference Implementation designation: no.

The canonical acceptance payload independently reproduces its recorded integrity identifier.

Acceptance supplies declaration authority only. It does not supply behaviour proof, deployment evidence, runtime evidence, evidence sufficiency, conformance, certification, security assessment, operational readiness, or protocol authority.

## 10. Supported-Relationship Boundary

The five supported transitions bind only:

- the declared public-input relationship;
- the proof relation under the supplied root;
- the bounded verifier result;
- proof-invalid prevention of satisfaction of the corresponding proof requirement;
- action-local uniqueness without participant identity;
- and proof verification as bounded component evidence.

They do not establish a complete applicable rule set, accepted-root evaluation, Protocol Action identity, authoritative Action admission, retry equivalence, complete Evaluation, Determination, or classification.

## 11. Accepted Non-Mapping Boundary

An accepted non-mapping bounds a row only where the formal row itself requires the exact separation or prohibition and the accepted declaration identifies the complete I164 evidence route.

No accepted non-mapping is used to prove positive existence, completeness, identity, correctness, behaviour, or satisfaction of another formal subject.

The ten non-transitioning non-mapping candidates remain explicit in the successor ledger with their exact unresolved dependency.

## 13. Action Coordinate and Public Inputs

`T-I164-ANONYMOUS-PUBLIC-ACTION` remains an implementation-owner mapping coordinate only. It is not a new protocol subject, Protocol Action occurrence identity, Private Actor identity, application replacement for Protocol Action, or source of protocol authority.

The accepted ordered public-input relationship remains exactly:

1. nullifier;
2. petitionId or actionId;
3. membershipRoot.

It remains separate from private witness, proof identity, Action occurrence identity, application target, UI request, relay request, retry, transaction, response, event, state mutation, reconciliation, and indexed record.

## 14. Root Boundary

The circuit establishes inclusion under the supplied root. `AnonymousMembershipRegistry.isValidRoot` exists independently. `PetitionRegistry.signPetitionWithZKProof` contains no call or state read establishing accepted-root status.

Therefore `U-ROOT` remains unresolved. This is not converted into a requirement result, defect, security finding, remediation instruction, or complete rule-set inference.

## 15. Evaluation, Determination, and Classification Boundaries

The preserved relationships are:

> proof verification `!=` complete Protocol Action Evaluation  
> partial or interrupted component sequence `!=` completed Evaluation  
> pre-evaluation or operational failure `!=` Protocol Action Determination

No fallthrough, revert, transaction, hash, receipt, relay response, mutation, event, reconciliation result, indexed record, aggregate, or application success state is selected as Determination.

Malformed submission, proof-generation failure, local preflight failure, freshness gating, executor failure, transport failure, fallback, submission failure, revert, uncertainty, reconciliation, persistence lag, and application failure remain non-classification outcomes unless a later exact accepted mapping establishes otherwise.

## 16. Evidence-Layer Separation

The following remain distinct:

- boundary documentation;
- tracked-direct evidence;
- reproducibly generated evidence;
- deployment-bound evidence;
- runtime-observation evidence;
- unavailable evidence;
- and evidence still required before assessment.

> evidence-path boundedness  
> `!=` evidence availability  
> `!=` evidence sufficiency  
> `!=` requirement result

A bounded evidence path identifies the required evidence route. It does not state that every required evidence item has been collected or is sufficient.

## 21. Assessment Readiness

Assessment readiness remains paused.

The readiness gate is:

> all applicable and permitted-choice rows have bounded evidence paths `->` requirement-level assessment may begin  
> any applicable or permitted-choice row remains unresolved `->` requirement-level assessment remains paused

There are 269 `still-ambiguous` rows, including all seven permitted-choice rows. Engineering[171] therefore does not authorize requirement-level assessment.

This record preserves:

> Private actors, public actions.
